   49: ## NEW Cardinal Rule for this runtime (cardinal-rule-6 fresh-from-github + official-native-channel)
   50: 
   51: 6. **Pull from newest GitHub before install via OFFICIAL NATIVE CHANNEL ONLY.** Every install command MUST first refresh upstream AND use the canonical official install primitive (no workarounds, no third-party mirrors, no shell-script wrappers around official commands):
   52:    - `npm install -g <pkg>@latest` from `https://registry.npmjs.org` (official npm registry; NOT pinned-old-version, NOT mirror)
   53:    - `gh release download --repo <owner>/<repo> $(gh release list --repo <owner>/<repo> --limit 1 --json tagName -q '.[0].tagName')` (official GitHub releases)
   54:    - `git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>.git <dest>` (fresh clone from canonical https://github.com URL) OR `git -C <dest> fetch origin && git -C <dest> reset --hard origin/main`
   55:    - `uvx --refresh <pkg>` (uvx auto-pulls latest from PyPI official)
   56:    - `docker pull <image>:latest` from official Docker Hub OR explicit registry
   57:    - `/plugin marketplace add <official-marketplace-url>` then `/plugin install <pkg>` (Anthropic-canonical marketplace mechanism)
   58:    - `cargo install <pkg>` from official crates.io
   59:    - NEVER copy from `Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/` (those may be stale per Marker Decay corollary at `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/evidence-policy.md` Marker Decay)
   60:    - NEVER use shell-script wrappers when official CLI primitive exists (e.g., do NOT write a `install-mcp.ps1` when `/plugin install` is the canonical Anthropic mechanism)

Cardinal-rule-11 META-process: every fire involving sibling-cite-import is gated by upstream-parity probe.
  154: 
  155:     ### CR-12 disposition lattice (5 classes — Wave 134 Fire 27 series codification)
  156: 
  157:     Beyond the **install-source priority order** (PRIMARY/SECONDARY/TERTIARY) above, every upstream-vs-incumbent relationship falls into ONE of 5 classes. Wave 134 Fire 27 series empirically mapped all 5:
  158: 
  159:     1. **GENUINELY-NEW** — no Anthropic/incumbent parallel exists for the primitive's scope. Disposition: **INSTALL via CR-12 PRIMARY path** (official-native-channel per CR-6). No further audit needed beyond Probe DAG.
  160: 
  161:     2. **DUPLICATE-FUNCTIONALITY** — full parallel exists in Anthropic/eee stack covering same scope and mechanism. Disposition: **REJECT-FOR-FIT** per `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/kiss-dry-yagni.md` Must-Never #4 (no duplicate functionality without clear reason).
  162: 
  163:     3. **PARTIAL-OVERLAP** — incumbent and candidate cover overlapping function via DIFFERENT MECHANISMS. Disposition: **CASE-BY-CASE**, typically CITE-PATTERN-ONLY or STUDY-PILOT-PATTERN-EXTRACT. Example: Wave 134 Fire 27-C mem0 (fact-extraction-based memory) vs eee graphiti (temporal-KG-based) vs mcp-memory (sqlite-vec-based) — same memory layer, different mechanisms. Codex T1 verdict at `.claude/state/codex_consult_w134_f27c_mem0_OUT.txt` STUDY-PILOT-PATTERN-EXTRACT conf=0.87.
  164: 
  165:     4. **PROVIDER-COMPLEMENT** — parallel API surfaces but different scopes; both can coexist. Disposition: **INSTALL as ALTERNATIVE (not PRIMARY)** — primary incumbent retains canonical position. Example: Wave 134 Fire 27-A openai-agents-python (vs Anthropic `claude-agent-sdk-python` @ HEAD `b512f256`) — Anthropic SDK = direct CC control plane (PRIMARY); OpenAI SDK = provider-agnostic orchestration (ALTERNATIVE). Codex T1 verdict at `.claude/state/codex_consult_w134_f27a_openai_agents_python_OUT.txt` STUDY-PILOT-PATTERN-EXTRACT conf=0.89.
  166: 
  167:     5. **ECOSYSTEM-IMPORT** — core primitive GENUINELY-NEW at the surface level but imports parallel ecosystem at the dependency level. Disposition: **EVALUATE ecosystem cost vs core primitive value** (often CITE-PATTERN-ONLY when ecosystem footprint disproportionate). Example: Wave 134 Fire 27-B langgraph (Pregel BSP graph execution is novel BUT requires `langchain-core>=1.4.0a2` + 6+ langgraph-* sub-package import + LangSmith proprietary recommended). Pattern B HNF disposition at `.claude/state/codex_consult_w134_f27b_langgraph_OUT.txt`.
  168: 
  169:     **Cite class for this 5-class lattice itself**: `constituents=[TIER-3-LOCAL-OPERATOR-DERIVED @ Wave 134 Fire 27-A + 27-B + 27-C codex T1 verdicts, TIER-2 @ existing cardinal-rule-12 scaffolding, TIER-2 @ Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/citation-discipline.md rule #8 source-class reduction lattice precedent, TIER-1-DIRECT @ Anthropic + OpenAI + LangChain TIER-1 maintainer evidence]; effective_tier=TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` per `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/citation-discipline.md` rule #8 MIN_PRECEDENCE.
  170: 
  171:     **Application discipline**:
  172:     - When auditing ANY upstream candidate for eee adoption, FIRST classify per 5-class lattice (after Probe DAG passes); THEN apply class-specific disposition
  173:     - Class assignment is determined by **mechanism comparison** vs incumbent (not surface API similarity alone)
  174:     - **PROVIDER-COMPLEMENT** vs **DUPLICATE-FUNCTIONALITY**: distinguished by whether both incumbents cover DIFFERENT scopes (PROVIDER-COMPLEMENT — both useful) or SAME scope (DUPLICATE — pick one)
  175:     - **PARTIAL-OVERLAP** vs **PROVIDER-COMPLEMENT**: distinguished by whether the DIFFERENT MECHANISMS suggest parallel co-existence (PROVIDER-COMPLEMENT) or competing-implementations (PARTIAL-OVERLAP requires case-by-case)
  176:     - **ECOSYSTEM-IMPORT** is the OPERATIONAL escape valve when a candidate's core primitive is novel but ecosystem footprint creates duplicate-frameworks concern
  177: 
  178:     **Sister-rule integration** (mapping CR-12 disposition lattice → `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md` verdict-shape lattice):
  179:     - GENUINELY-NEW → APPROVE / STUDY-PILOT-NARROW
  180:     - DUPLICATE-FUNCTIONALITY → REJECT-FOR-FIT
  181:     - PARTIAL-OVERLAP → CITE-PATTERN-ONLY / STUDY-PILOT-PATTERN-EXTRACT (typical)
  182:     - PROVIDER-COMPLEMENT → STUDY-PILOT-PATTERN-EXTRACT (typical) OR STUDY-PILOT-NARROW
  183:     - ECOSYSTEM-IMPORT → CITE-PATTERN-ONLY (typical) OR STUDY-PILOT-NARROW-WITH-VENV-ISOLATION
  184: 
  185:     Cite anchor: Wave 134 Fire 27-A + 27-B + 27-C empirical mapping; codification ship W134-F27-RESEARCH-ARCH-D 2026-05-10; Pattern B HNF disposition on codification cross-model gate per `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Pattern B` (codex T1 spent 300s budget on extensive filesystem exploration of prior Wave 134 fire deliverables; no terminal JSON verdict; ship-as-designed disposition per Pattern B + T3 post-commit verification gate).
  186:

  271: ### The 4-skill stack — meta-skills active in this runtime
  272: 
  273: This runtime runs with 4 meta-skills active that govern multi-skill orchestration. They auto-fire per their `description:` triggers — no manual invocation required for the meta-layer to activate. Skill discovery is Anthropic CC native mechanism (per `https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills`) — Claude reads `description:` frontmatter of every available SKILL.md and invokes via the `Skill` tool when description matches the current task.
  274: 
  275: | # | Meta-skill | Source plugin | Role |
  276: |---|---|---|---|
  277: | 1 | `using-superpowers` | superpowers@claude-plugins-official | "1% rule" — invoke any skill that has even a 1% chance of applying. Defines Skill Priority (Process skills first / Implementation skills second). Red Flags table catches rationalizations. |
  278: | 2 | `using-agent-skills` | agent-skills@addy-agent-skills | Discovery + invocation governance for the 21 addy engineering-phase skills. Auto-fires at session start to surface relevant skill for current task. |
  279: | 3 | `skill-comply` | everything-claude-code | Post-invocation verification that skill use was correct + complete (verifies skill checklist completion if skill body had one). |
  280: | 4 | `skill-creator` | skill-creator@claude-plugins-official | Authoring loop: draft → eval → iterate → benchmark → optimize description for triggering accuracy. Use when creating new skills OR when an existing skill should be improved. |
  281: 
  282: ### How and when to invoke skills (mandatory discipline)
  283: 
  284: **For Claude (main thread)**:
  285: - Per `using-superpowers` 1% rule: invoke ANY relevant skill BEFORE response or action. Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions / file exploration / planning.
  286: - Multiple skills: Process skills (brainstorming / debugging / verification-before-completion) determine HOW; Implementation skills (frontend-design / api-and-interface-design / TDD) guide EXECUTION. Layer them when both apply.
  287: - Announce: "Using [skill] to [purpose]" before each invocation per `using-superpowers` skill flow digraph.
  288: - Follow skill EXACTLY when rigid (TDD / debugging); adapt principles when flexible (patterns).
  289: 
  290: **For subagents** (per CCBP `claude-subagents.md:32-34` `skills:` preload field):
  291: - Subagent briefs MUST include skill-awareness clause when role demands. Standing directive `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md` invariant applies — agent briefs cite TIER-1 sources at file:line + HEAD SHA, including relevant SKILL.md files for the agent's role.
  292: - Subagent frontmatter `skills:` field can preload specific skills (CC 2.1+); `allowed-tools` controls tool surface; `context: fork` runs in isolated subagent context per CCBP 15-field spec.
  293: - Common preload pairings:
  294:   - `sota-researcher` agent → preload `deep-research` + `research-ops` ECC skills + `iterative-retrieval` + `using-agent-skills` (auto-fires regardless via discovery)
  295:   - `architect` agent → preload `code-architect` + `agent-harness-construction` + `agentic-engineering` ECC skills
  296:   - `code-reviewer` agent → preload `code-review-and-quality` (addy) + `silent-failure-hunter` (ECC) + `requesting-code-review` / `receiving-code-review` (superpowers)
  297:   - `debugger` agent → preload `systematic-debugging` (superpowers) + `debugging-and-error-recovery` (addy) + `agent-introspection-debugging` (ECC)
  298:   - `verifier` agent → preload `verification-before-completion` (superpowers) + `eval-harness` (ECC)
  299: 
  300: **For multi-skill orchestration** (chained or layered):
  301: - **Chain example**: brainstorming → writing-plans → subagent-driven-development → test-driven-development → requesting-code-review → finishing-a-development-branch (per superpowers workflow grammar L7 sequence)
  302: - **Layer example**: while implementing a feature: `feature-dev` (claude-plugins-official) PLUS `incremental-implementation` (addy) PLUS `tdd` (superpowers) PLUS `safety-guard` (ECC) PLUS `verification-before-completion` (superpowers)
  303: - **Anti-pattern**: invoking ONLY one skill when 2-4 apply. The 1% rule is bidirectional — even if uncertain whether skill X applies, invoke it; if it turns out wrong, drop it.
  304:

  318: ### Update triggers
  319: 
  320: Re-evaluate this skill orchestration discipline when:
  321: - A 5th meta-skill emerges in a registered marketplace (e.g., `using-mcp-skills` or `using-claude-md-skills`) — add to the 4-skill stack
  322: - Anthropic CC ships a `skills` runtime probe primitive (e.g., a `claude skills list` CLI subcommand or SessionStart auto-injection) that obviates manual `using-agent-skills` auto-fire
  323: - A subagent class consistently fails to invoke a relevant skill — promote that skill into the agent's frontmatter `skills:` preload
  324: - A documented skill conflict surfaces between auto-firing meta-skills (e.g., `using-superpowers` + `using-agent-skills` both demand precedence) — codify priority order in superpowers/using-superpowers Red Flags table
  325: - A new SOTA repo ships a competing skill-orchestration discipline (e.g., openai/codex-skills) — re-audit Axis 1 multi-org convergence per `convergence-gate.md`

  277: 
  278: ## Promotion gates (when does a row advance status)
  279: 
  280: - `PLANNED` → `STAGED`: install command run, output captured to `install-provenance.md`
  281: - `STAGED` → `INSTALLED-AMBER`: smoke probe completed but Probe DAG flagged P2/P3
  282: - `STAGED` → `INSTALLED`: smoke probe + axis-1+2+3 PASS + Probe DAG clean
  283: - `STAGED` → `REJECTED-POST-PROBE`: axis-fail or Probe DAG REJECT
  284: - `STAGED` → `DEFERRED-PENDING-FIX`: upstream issue blocks install; cite-only retained as substitute
  285: - `INSTALLED-AMBER` → `INSTALLED`: deferred fix landed
  286: - ANY → re-evaluation triggered per `docs/install-from-github-discipline.md §"When to re-install"`
  287: 
  288: ### `@latest` resolution at execute time (per Wave 50 fire 8 Pattern A on Agent I F-4 — operationalizes CR-9 version-pin mandate)
  289: 
  290: Every install command in this manifest tagged `@latest` (e.g., `npm install -g @doobidoo/mcp-memory-service@latest`, `docker pull qdrant/qdrant:latest`, `npm install -g markdownlint-cli2@latest`) carries **implicit `@latest-acknowledged-D6-risk:2026-05-06` status** — the manifest planning intent is "pull newest at install execution per CR-6, with explicit D6 today-release-auto-upgrade risk acknowledgment per CR-9". At install execution, the operator MUST:
  291: 
  292: 1. Resolve the actual version (e.g., `npm view @doobidoo/mcp-memory-service version` returns `1.4.7` → install command becomes `npm install -g @doobidoo/mcp-memory-service@1.4.7`)
  293: 2. Record both the `@latest` planning intent AND the resolved `@<resolved-version>` in `docs/install-provenance.md` under the install row (per CR-6 D6 risk-acknowledged contract)
  294: 3. Pin the manifest install command to the resolved version post-install (manifest row updated from `@latest` to `@<resolved-version>` as part of `STAGED → INSTALLED` promotion)
  295: 4. Re-evaluation trigger: per `docs/install-from-github-discipline.md §"When to re-install"` — the resolved-version pin holds until either a re-install is needed (per upstream changelog or sibling D6 evidence) OR the row's manifest age exceeds N-day re-evaluation cadence
  296: 
  297: **Rationale**: the manifest's `@latest` install commands ARE the SOTA-discipline contract per CR-6 fresh-from-github (do not pre-pin to stale versions in the planning document). The resolution-at-execute-time + provenance-recording gate satisfies CR-9 version-pin mandate without violating CR-6 fresh-from-github. The promotion-gate state machine above enforces this — a row cannot advance `STAGED → INSTALLED` until step 3 (resolved-version pin) lands in the manifest row.
  298: 
  299: **Scope**: applies to ALL install commands carrying raw `@latest` AND `@HEAD`-equivalent pulls (`git clone --depth 1 ... origin/main` without SHA pin). Does NOT apply to `@<version-tag>` pinned install commands (those satisfy CR-9 directly).

  575: ### 18.2 Research-then-install workflow (operational shape)
  576: 
  577: For every install ambiguity (which version to pin / which channel / which install pattern / unknown error class):
  578: 
  579: ```
  580: 1. Encounter ambiguity (build error, version conflict, install path unknown, etc.)
  581: 2. INVOKE sota-researcher subagent with explicit scope:
  582:    - probe upstream repo + alternative repos via mcp__github__search_repositories (axis-1 maintainership filter)
  583:    - probe CCBP cite anchors at Z:/repos/deps/claude-code-best-practice-shan/
  584:    - probe Wave 47 GRAND CATALOG at Z:/claude-sota/tmp/wave47-grand-catalog-2026-05-06.md (934 repos / 125 perfect-convergence baseline)
  585:    - probe sibling claude-sota memory entries at Z:/claude-sota/.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota/memory/ for prior-decisions
  586: 3. CLASSIFY return per cardinal-rule-10 priority order:
  587:    (a) Install canonical SOTA solution → execute install per Pattern 1-6
  588:    (b) Cite-adapt SOTA pattern → cite-anchor + adapt with cardinal-rule-8 conformance
  589:    (c) Best SOTA-recommended approach → operator gate + axis-1+2+3 verdict
  590:    (d) HONEST-NON-FINDING → escalate to operator
  591: 4. LOG to install-provenance.md with research provenance (sota-researcher invocation + return + classification + decision)
  592: ```

290:Every install command in this manifest tagged `@latest` (e.g., `npm install -g @doobidoo/mcp-memory-service@latest`, `docker pull qdrant/qdrant:latest`, `npm install -g markdownlint-cli2@latest`) carries **implicit `@latest-acknowledged-D6-risk:2026-05-06` status** — the manifest planning intent is "pull newest at install execution per CR-6, with explicit D6 today-release-auto-upgrade risk acknowledgment per CR-9". At install execution, the operator MUST:
292:1. Resolve the actual version (e.g., `npm view @doobidoo/mcp-memory-service version` returns `1.4.7` → install command becomes `npm install -g @doobidoo/mcp-memory-service@1.4.7`)
294:3. Pin the manifest install command to the resolved version post-install (manifest row updated from `@latest` to `@<resolved-version>` as part of `STAGED → INSTALLED` promotion)
295:4. Re-evaluation trigger: per `docs/install-from-github-discipline.md §"When to re-install"` — the resolved-version pin holds until either a re-install is needed (per upstream changelog or sibling D6 evidence) OR the row's manifest age exceeds N-day re-evaluation cadence
297:**Rationale**: the manifest's `@latest` install commands ARE the SOTA-discipline contract per CR-6 fresh-from-github (do not pre-pin to stale versions in the planning document). The resolution-at-execute-time + provenance-recording gate satisfies CR-9 version-pin mandate without violating CR-6 fresh-from-github. The promotion-gate state machine above enforces this — a row cannot advance `STAGED → INSTALLED` until step 3 (resolved-version pin) lands in the manifest row.

  277: 
  278: ## Promotion gates (when does a row advance status)
  279: 
  280: - `PLANNED` → `STAGED`: install command run, output captured to `install-provenance.md`
  281: - `STAGED` → `INSTALLED-AMBER`: smoke probe completed but Probe DAG flagged P2/P3
  282: - `STAGED` → `INSTALLED`: smoke probe + axis-1+2+3 PASS + Probe DAG clean
  283: - `STAGED` → `REJECTED-POST-PROBE`: axis-fail or Probe DAG REJECT
  284: - `STAGED` → `DEFERRED-PENDING-FIX`: upstream issue blocks install; cite-only retained as substitute
  285: - `INSTALLED-AMBER` → `INSTALLED`: deferred fix landed
  286: - ANY → re-evaluation triggered per `docs/install-from-github-discipline.md §"When to re-install"`
  287: 
  288: ### `@latest` resolution at execute time (per Wave 50 fire 8 Pattern A on Agent I F-4 — operationalizes CR-9 version-pin mandate)
  289: 
  290: Every install command in this manifest tagged `@latest` (e.g., `npm install -g @doobidoo/mcp-memory-service@latest`, `docker pull qdrant/qdrant:latest`, `npm install -g markdownlint-cli2@latest`) carries **implicit `@latest-acknowledged-D6-risk:2026-05-06` status** — the manifest planning intent is "pull newest at install execution per CR-6, with explicit D6 today-release-auto-upgrade risk acknowledgment per CR-9". At install execution, the operator MUST:
  291: 
  292: 1. Resolve the actual version (e.g., `npm view @doobidoo/mcp-memory-service version` returns `1.4.7` → install command becomes `npm install -g @doobidoo/mcp-memory-service@1.4.7`)
  293: 2. Record both the `@latest` planning intent AND the resolved `@<resolved-version>` in `docs/install-provenance.md` under the install row (per CR-6 D6 risk-acknowledged contract)
  294: 3. Pin the manifest install command to the resolved version post-install (manifest row updated from `@latest` to `@<resolved-version>` as part of `STAGED → INSTALLED` promotion)
  295: 4. Re-evaluation trigger: per `docs/install-from-github-discipline.md §"When to re-install"` — the resolved-version pin holds until either a re-install is needed (per upstream changelog or sibling D6 evidence) OR the row's manifest age exceeds N-day re-evaluation cadence
  296: 
  297: **Rationale**: the manifest's `@latest` install commands ARE the SOTA-discipline contract per CR-6 fresh-from-github (do not pre-pin to stale versions in the planning document). The resolution-at-execute-time + provenance-recording gate satisfies CR-9 version-pin mandate without violating CR-6 fresh-from-github. The promotion-gate state machine above enforces this — a row cannot advance `STAGED → INSTALLED` until step 3 (resolved-version pin) lands in the manifest row.
  298: 
  299: **Scope**: applies to ALL install commands carrying raw `@latest` AND `@HEAD`-equivalent pulls (`git clone --depth 1 ... origin/main` without SHA pin). Does NOT apply to `@<version-tag>` pinned install commands (those satisfy CR-9 directly).

  556: ## Section 18 — Research Architecture (NEW per cardinal-rule-10 user-trigger 2026-05-06)
  557:

  560: ### 18.1 Research-first install order amendment (modifies Agent E Tier order)
  561: 
  562: Per cardinal-rule-10, sota-researcher subagent install promotes from Tier 5 (Agent E original) → **Tier 1** (immediately after codex CLI + before any non-foundational install):
  563: 
  564: | Tier | Install (enumerated rows) | Rationale |

  586: 3. CLASSIFY return per cardinal-rule-10 priority order:
  587:    (a) Install canonical SOTA solution → execute install per Pattern 1-6

  591: 4. LOG to install-provenance.md with research provenance (sota-researcher invocation + return + classification + decision)
  592: ```

  104: ## NEW Cardinal Rule for this runtime (cardinal-rule-10 research-first-then-install)
  105: 
  106: 10. **Research-first-then-install — every build/error/unknown encountered in this runtime triggers SOTA research BEFORE remediation.** When claude-sota-installed hits a build failure / dependency error / unknown configuration / install ambiguity / new feature requirement, the FIRST action is SOTA research (find canonical repo / pattern / cite anchor that solves the class) BEFORE attempting fix. Remediation paths in priority order:
  107:     - **(a) Install canonical SOTA solution** — research surfaces an upstream repo that solves the class (e.g., FM-17.d watchdog stall → install `fm17d_stall_detector.py` from sibling cite-import per Section 13 — corrected from prior stale "Section 13.1" reference per Wave 50 fire 10 Pattern A on Agent I F-14 + Agent L Dimension 3 P3)
  108:     - **(b) Cite-adapt SOTA pattern** — research surfaces a SOTA pattern that doesn't have a single install-class implementation; adapt the pattern with file:line cite anchor per cardinal-rule-8 (e.g., superpowers `subagent-driven-development` 2-stage review pattern)
  109:     - **(c) Best SOTA-recommended approach** — research surfaces no single install-class repo or pattern; operator picks the best SOTA-recommended approach with explicit research provenance + axis-1+2+3 verdict
  110:     - **(d) HONEST-NON-FINDING** — research returns no SOTA solution; classify as GENUINE-GAP per `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories`, document in `docs/install-provenance.md`, escalate to operator decision
  111: 
  112:     **Implication for runtime architecture**: sota-researcher subagent MUST install in **Tier 1** (not Tier 5 per Agent E install order) — it's the first non-foundational install because it drives every subsequent install decision. sota-researcher is the load-bearing primitive enabling cardinal-rule-10 conformance. See manifest §Section 18 — Research Architecture for full design.
  113: 
  114:     **Build-error remediation specifics**: when codex T1 / T2 / T3 returns NEEDS-REVISION with prescribed-edits referring to upstream APIs, libraries, patterns the runtime doesn't have installed yet — DO NOT hand-code the fix. When the failure needs an independent Codex worker, first route through `/codex:rescue --background <task>` or `/codex:rescue --wait <task>` from `codex@openai-codex@1.0.4`; if the Claude Agent path hits the known FM-17.f 1M-context blocker, fall back to direct `codex exec` foreground+tee.   154: 
  155:     ### CR-12 disposition lattice (5 classes — Wave 134 Fire 27 series codification)
  156: 
  157:     Beyond the **install-source priority order** (PRIMARY/SECONDARY/TERTIARY) above, every upstream-vs-incumbent relationship falls into ONE of 5 classes. Wave 134 Fire 27 series empirically mapped all 5:
  158: 
  159:     1. **GENUINELY-NEW** — no Anthropic/incumbent parallel exists for the primitive's scope. Disposition: **INSTALL via CR-12 PRIMARY path** (official-native-channel per CR-6). No further audit needed beyond Probe DAG.
  160: 
  161:     2. **DUPLICATE-FUNCTIONALITY** — full parallel exists in Anthropic/eee stack covering same scope and mechanism. Disposition: **REJECT-FOR-FIT** per `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/kiss-dry-yagni.md` Must-Never #4 (no duplicate functionality without clear reason).
  162: 
  163:     3. **PARTIAL-OVERLAP** — incumbent and candidate cover overlapping function via DIFFERENT MECHANISMS. Disposition: **CASE-BY-CASE**, typically CITE-PATTERN-ONLY or STUDY-PILOT-PATTERN-EXTRACT. Example: Wave 134 Fire 27-C mem0 (fact-extraction-based memory) vs eee graphiti (temporal-KG-based) vs mcp-memory (sqlite-vec-based) — same memory layer, different mechanisms. Codex T1 verdict at `.claude/state/codex_consult_w134_f27c_mem0_OUT.txt` STUDY-PILOT-PATTERN-EXTRACT conf=0.87.
  164: 
  165:     4. **PROVIDER-COMPLEMENT** — parallel API surfaces but different scopes; both can coexist. Disposition: **INSTALL as ALTERNATIVE (not PRIMARY)** — primary incumbent retains canonical position. Example: Wave 134 Fire 27-A openai-agents-python (vs Anthropic `claude-agent-sdk-python` @ HEAD `b512f256`) — Anthropic SDK = direct CC control plane (PRIMARY); OpenAI SDK = provider-agnostic orchestration (ALTERNATIVE). Codex T1 verdict at `.claude/state/codex_consult_w134_f27a_openai_agents_python_OUT.txt` STUDY-PILOT-PATTERN-EXTRACT conf=0.89.
  166: 
  167:     5. **ECOSYSTEM-IMPORT** — core primitive GENUINELY-NEW at the surface level but imports parallel ecosystem at the dependency level. Disposition: **EVALUATE ecosystem cost vs core primitive value** (often CITE-PATTERN-ONLY when ecosystem footprint disproportionate). Example: Wave 134 Fire 27-B langgraph (Pregel BSP graph execution is novel BUT requires `langchain-core>=1.4.0a2` + 6+ langgraph-* sub-package import + LangSmith proprietary recommended). Pattern B HNF disposition at `.claude/state/codex_consult_w134_f27b_langgraph_OUT.txt`.
  168: 
  169:     **Cite class for this 5-class lattice itself**: `constituents=[TIER-3-LOCAL-OPERATOR-DERIVED @ Wave 134 Fire 27-A + 27-B + 27-C codex T1 verdicts, TIER-2 @ existing cardinal-rule-12 scaffolding, TIER-2 @ Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/citation-discipline.md rule #8 source-class reduction lattice precedent, TIER-1-DIRECT @ Anthropic + OpenAI + LangChain TIER-1 maintainer evidence]; effective_tier=TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` per `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/citation-discipline.md` rule #8 MIN_PRECEDENCE.
  170: 
  171:     **Application discipline**:
  172:     - When auditing ANY upstream candidate for eee adoption, FIRST classify per 5-class lattice (after Probe DAG passes); THEN apply class-specific disposition
  173:     - Class assignment is determined by **mechanism comparison** vs incumbent (not surface API similarity alone)
  174:     - **PROVIDER-COMPLEMENT** vs **DUPLICATE-FUNCTIONALITY**: distinguished by whether both incumbents cover DIFFERENT scopes (PROVIDER-COMPLEMENT — both useful) or SAME scope (DUPLICATE — pick one)
  175:     - **PARTIAL-OVERLAP** vs **PROVIDER-COMPLEMENT**: distinguished by whether the DIFFERENT MECHANISMS suggest parallel co-existence (PROVIDER-COMPLEMENT) or competing-implementations (PARTIAL-OVERLAP requires case-by-case)
  176:     - **ECOSYSTEM-IMPORT** is the OPERATIONAL escape valve when a candidate's core primitive is novel but ecosystem footprint creates duplicate-frameworks concern
  177: 
  178:     **Sister-rule integration** (mapping CR-12 disposition lattice → `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md` verdict-shape lattice):
  179:     - GENUINELY-NEW → APPROVE / STUDY-PILOT-NARROW
  180:     - DUPLICATE-FUNCTIONALITY → REJECT-FOR-FIT
  181:     - PARTIAL-OVERLAP → CITE-PATTERN-ONLY / STUDY-PILOT-PATTERN-EXTRACT (typical)
  182:     - PROVIDER-COMPLEMENT → STUDY-PILOT-PATTERN-EXTRACT (typical) OR STUDY-PILOT-NARROW
  183:     - ECOSYSTEM-IMPORT → CITE-PATTERN-ONLY (typical) OR STUDY-PILOT-NARROW-WITH-VENV-ISOLATION
  184: 
  185:     Cite anchor: Wave 134 Fire 27-A + 27-B + 27-C empirical mapping; codification ship W134-F27-RESEARCH-ARCH-D 2026-05-10; Pattern B HNF disposition on codification cross-model gate per `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Pattern B` (codex T1 spent 300s budget on extensive filesystem exploration of prior Wave 134 fire deliverables; no terminal JSON verdict; ship-as-designed disposition per Pattern B + T3 post-commit verification gate).
  186: 
  187:     **Wave 50 fire 9 Agent J upstream-parity verdict (PROVISIONAL — drives fire 11 Section 14/14.5/13 row rewrites)**:
  188:     - **Path A UPSTREAM-INSTALL-AVAILABLE (×2)**: codex-rescue subagent → `/plugin marketplace add openai/codex-plugin-cc && /plugin install codex@openai-codex@1.0.4` + safety_guard.py hook → `/plugin install everything-claude-code@2.0.0-rc.1`

  271: ### The 4-skill stack — meta-skills active in this runtime
  272: 
  273: This runtime runs with 4 meta-skills active that govern multi-skill orchestration. They auto-fire per their `description:` triggers — no manual invocation required for the meta-layer to activate. Skill discovery is Anthropic CC native mechanism (per `https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills`) — Claude reads `description:` frontmatter of every available SKILL.md and invokes via the `Skill` tool when description matches the current task.
  274: 
  275: | # | Meta-skill | Source plugin | Role |
  276: |---|---|---|---|
  277: | 1 | `using-superpowers` | superpowers@claude-plugins-official | "1% rule" — invoke any skill that has even a 1% chance of applying. Defines Skill Priority (Process skills first / Implementation skills second). Red Flags table catches rationalizations. |
  278: | 2 | `using-agent-skills` | agent-skills@addy-agent-skills | Discovery + invocation governance for the 21 addy engineering-phase skills. Auto-fires at session start to surface relevant skill for current task. |
  279: | 3 | `skill-comply` | everything-claude-code | Post-invocation verification that skill use was correct + complete (verifies skill checklist completion if skill body had one). |
  280: | 4 | `skill-creator` | skill-creator@claude-plugins-official | Authoring loop: draft → eval → iterate → benchmark → optimize description for triggering accuracy. Use when creating new skills OR when an existing skill should be improved. |
  281: 
  282: ### How and when to invoke skills (mandatory discipline)
  283: 
  284: **For Claude (main thread)**:
  285: - Per `using-superpowers` 1% rule: invoke ANY relevant skill BEFORE response or action. Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions / file exploration / planning.
  286: - Multiple skills: Process skills (brainstorming / debugging / verification-before-completion) determine HOW; Implementation skills (frontend-design / api-and-interface-design / TDD) guide EXECUTION. Layer them when both apply.
  287: - Announce: "Using [skill] to [purpose]" before each invocation per `using-superpowers` skill flow digraph.
  288: - Follow skill EXACTLY when rigid (TDD / debugging); adapt principles when flexible (patterns).
  289: 
  290: **For subagents** (per CCBP `claude-subagents.md:32-34` `skills:` preload field):
  291: - Subagent briefs MUST include skill-awareness clause when role demands. Standing directive `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md` invariant applies — agent briefs cite TIER-1 sources at file:line + HEAD SHA, including relevant SKILL.md files for the agent's role.
  292: - Subagent frontmatter `skills:` field can preload specific skills (CC 2.1+); `allowed-tools` controls tool surface; `context: fork` runs in isolated subagent context per CCBP 15-field spec.
  293: - Common preload pairings:

  301: - **Chain example**: brainstorming → writing-plans → subagent-driven-development → test-driven-development → requesting-code-review → finishing-a-development-branch (per superpowers workflow grammar L7 sequence)
  302: - **Layer example**: while implementing a feature: `feature-dev` (claude-plugins-official) PLUS `incremental-implementation` (addy) PLUS `tdd` (superpowers) PLUS `safety-guard` (ECC) PLUS `verification-before-completion` (superpowers)
  303: - **Anti-pattern**: invoking ONLY one skill when 2-4 apply. The 1% rule is bidirectional — even if uncertain whether skill X applies, invoke it; if it turns out wrong, drop it.
